### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 中國記憶體技術突破、通膨升溫、AI融資擔憂, 形成亞股大跌風暴: AI融資及過度擴產的擔憂甚囂塵上,
加上殖利率攀升, 提高市場評價重調並放大檢視AI利空, 形成亞股重挫主因。
• 9月通膨超預期升溫, 市場擔憂10月再升1碼: CPI月增率創5個月來新高, 美債殖利率走高, 儘管聯準會
官員暗示10月無升息意願, 但市場超前反應升息可能, 使殖利率higher-for-longer。
• Q3財報季開跑, 預估獲利年增二成以上: 金融股率先公布Q3財報, 殖利率創高削弱固定收益交易收入,
但貸款需求穩定、景氣熱絡, 6大銀行營收成長仍創近年新高, 預估Q3標普500企業獲利年增逾二成。
• 台積電財報優於預期, 強調無過度投資疑慮: Q3營收獲利優於預期, 毛利率符合預期, 但上調全年預估
營收, 並強調CSP與資料中心無過度投資或擴產疑慮, 2026-2028年資本支出將遠高於過去三年。
2. 後續看點:
• 四大CSP財報(10/27-10/28)、FOMC利率會議(10/29)、ECB政策會議(10/29)、台灣GDP(10/30)

策略思維
1. 股市−多頭不變, 但高利率增添評價波動: 殖利率震盪上揚壓力增加, 市場持續調整股市評價面, 使股價落後
基本面表現。不過, AI趨勢明確, 企業營收獲利未來成長空間仍大, 應逢評價仍低時擴大布局。
2. 債市−長端不確定性仍高, 宜以中短天期或公司債為佳: 通膨高於預期, 長率仍有上行壓力。建議控制債券部
位存續期間, 選擇短天期或浮動債較佳。
3. 匯市−美元走揚與股市震盪, 引發台幣重貶: 美元雙率走高提高外資賣超台股疑慮, 台幣可能須承受額外壓力。

Cathay’s View
債務疑慮, 驅動CoreWeave債券利率飆升 大型CSP債券利率仍偏穩定
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 美國9月通膨數據高於預期, 推升美債殖利率一度急彈, 進而引發市場對AI融資風險的疑慮。與此同時, 馬斯克及部
分創投人士也注意到CoreWeave債券殖利率飆升的問題, 並示警相關風險升高, 導致CoreWeave股價重挫15%。
• 不過, 對谷歌、亞馬遜、微軟等大型CSP業者而言, 儘管仍面臨現金流轉負的壓力, 但其發行的公司債價格仍相對
穩定, 並未因為CoreWeave事件而同步走高 , 反而逆勢走低 。這顯示, 目前AI融資疑慮仍主要集中在規模較小的
CSP業者, 尚未全面擴散至大型業者。然而, 隨著美債利率維持高檔, AI資本支出的融資壓力仍可能持續擾動市場。
科技｜CoreWeave債券利率飆升, 再度觸發AI融資敏感神經

### Page 6

6
Cathay’s View
• 隨美國10年公債殖利率站穩5%, 市場對AI過度投資的擔憂再起, 掀起AI拋售潮。但從產業基本面觀察, 四大CSP資
本支出仍在成長, 僅是年增率放緩, 台積電法說會亦表示沒有見到任何AI需求放緩的跡象, 意味即使AI成長放緩, 也
不代表缺少成長動能, 而是從初期萌發階段, 進入穩定成長期。
• 此外, 即便企業獲利增速放緩, 但只要能保持正成長, 普遍都可帶動指數上漲。以美台股市為例, 2021Q2為企業獲
利成長高峰, 但至2022Q3後股市才因升息與獲利轉負而下跌。近期股市大跌主要受市場重新調整估值與AI融資疑
慮影響, AI產業前景與企業基本面無虞, 逢拉回應站買方思考。
2027年CSP資本支出增速放緩, 但仍將保持成長

Cathay’s View
銀行股Q3財報狀況 摩根大通利息收入穩定, 但非利息收入受到債市拖累
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3財報。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3財報。
• 美國6大銀行Q3獲利仍具韌性, 其中放貸需求穩定成長, 但固定收益交易動能受到拖累。以商業銀行摩根大通為例,
放款總額成長10%至1.5兆美元, 存款亦有9%的成長, 使得利息收入270億美元, 年增12%, 為近10季最強動能。不
過, 由於美債利率高漲 , 固定收益交易收入年減 4%, 使得非利息收入 Q3僅成長3%。Q3整體收入為 502億美元,
EPS為5.57美元, 不及上季, 但仍創下同期新高。
• 就投資銀行高盛而言, 股票交易收入年增72%, 但固定收益的收入也年減1%, 使得整體收入為183億美元, 雖然仍有
28%的成長, 但動能也不及上季35%。摩根史坦利亦然, 手續費收入從上季的28%降至6%。
美股｜經濟保持韌性使利息收入穩定成長, 美債利率高漲影響固定收益收入, 使銀行財報表現分化

Cathay’s View
9月油價反彈, 同步推升CPI走揚 除能源反彈強勁, 商品與服務同步上升
註: 西德州原油(WTI)採每月均值之年增率計算。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9、2026/10。
註: 超級核心通膨為服務扣除租金。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 9月CPI年增率3.7%、核心CPI年增率2.6%雙雙高於預期(3.6%/2.5%)。受美伊衝突影響, 西德州原油(WTI)月均價
由8月82.5美元/桶升至9月95.7美元/桶, 推升汽油價格月增9.1%。此外, 核心商品、核心服務月增率均較前月升高;
細項方面, 租金(+0.4%)、醫療保健(+0.3%)、運輸(+0.6%)、二手車(+0.7%)升幅較大, 通膨壓力已非侷限於能源。
• 物價壓力由「單一油價衝擊」進一步擴散至商品與服務端 , 致使整體通膨降溫進程停滯 。在通膨黏性增強 、第二
輪傳導效應持續累積下, 若美伊情勢持續緊張, 高通膨維持時間將被迫拉長, 亦使美債利率持續面臨上行壓力。

通膨｜9月CPI升幅超預期, 能源供給壓力逐漸擴散

### Page 9

Cathay’s View
CPI公布後, 升息預期先升後降 期限溢價同步推升10年美債利率
註: 聯邦利率期貨預估至2027年底約升息3碼。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/15。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 9月CPI超預期, 利率先升後降: 10月升息機率一度上升至50%, 2年、10年債利率一度突破4.9%、5.3%。CPI公布
後, Fed官員仍對通膨保持警戒 , 不過Bowman表示無須立即行動 、Daly認為無需倉促決定升息 、Goolsbee與
Barkin傾向觀察更多數據; 官員談話緩和連續升息疑慮, 牽動10月升息機率回落至27%, 美債殖利率自高點回落。
• 通膨壓力未解, 升息預期將給予短端利率支撐; 長端利率則進一步受日漸墊高的期限溢價影響, 推升利率中樞水平
上移。中長期而言, 除了升息路徑的不確定性, 在財政赤字惡化、AI融資推升發債需求的背景下, 高利率環境仍將
成為市場常態。
利率｜短線升息預期反覆, 惟長端利率中樞仍偏高

高利率推升民生信用壓力
註: 嚴重拖欠率係指90天以上逾期(90+Delinquency)。
資料來源: Bloomberg, NY Fed, 截至2026/10、2026Q2。

密大消費者信心

購車
• 高利環境推升民生融資壓力, 信用卡、車貸及房貸融資成本增加, 恐使拖欠率延續持續走升。此外, 亦影響耐久財

消費動能: 1)30年期房貸固定利率突破7.4%, 導致購屋與換屋門檻提高; 根據房地產經紀公司Redfin, 美國9月成屋
待售簽約量月減4%, 創2023年以來最大單月降幅, 待售房屋供給則升至2020年以來高點。2)油價走高亦增添購車
負擔, 壓縮相關消費意願。
• 本輪通膨源自能源供給、供應鏈重組等因素, 因此升息雖有助抑制需求, 但也將加重消費成本。未來若高利環境持
續影響家計單位消費, 將使本已分化的需求結構進一步惡化 , 加劇美國K型消費與資產表現分化 。即便Fed維持緊
縮立場, 但除了在控制通膨風險以外, 亦需兼顧是否抑制經濟成長、需求降溫過快的風險。

### Page 11

Cathay’s View
賭盤顯示民主黨贏得兩院選舉可能較大 政策情境分析: 若民主黨兩院勝選
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: Morgan Stanley, 國泰世華銀行整理。
• 美國期中選舉將於11/3舉行, 改選參眾議院與州長。由於油價大漲、通膨仍高, 共和黨與川普的民眾支持度持續下
降, 賭盤預期民主黨全面橫掃兩院的可能性上升, 扭轉目前由共和黨掌握兩院的局面。
• 若民主黨贏得兩院, 將提高國會制衡力道, 美伊衝突在選後全面開戰的可能下降; 其次, 財政政策將從減稅轉向補貼,
惟財政赤字是否改善仍須觀察; 值得留意的是, 若民主黨取得兩院與多數州長勝選, 可能提高資料中心反對建設聲
浪, 影響投資人信心。
• 然而, 並非所有共和黨都鼓勵資料中心。握有全美25%算力的德州州長Abbott有望連任, 雖是共和黨, 但政策已轉
向提高資料中心建設限制, 顯示當地資源緊迫性提升。
期中選舉｜民主黨橫掃兩院可能上升, 可能降低美伊再次開戰、但提高資料中心反彈聲浪

政策/市場 影響
關稅、移民政策 屬於總統行政權, 不受影響
美伊衝突 選後全面開戰可能下降
財政赤字 赤字持平或小幅擴張
資料中心建設 反對資料中心建設聲浪可能提高
股市 資料中心建設進度若延後將造成科技股震盪, 但基本面不受政策影響
債市 通膨、Fed政策立場影響較大20

EPS創新高, 毛利率保持在65~67%高檔 2027年預估資本支出上看850億美元
資料來源: Bloomberg預估、台積電預估。預估期間為2026Q4。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估。預估期間為2026-2027年。
• 2026年Q3 EPS 29.43元, 年增68.8%, 營收年增41.3%, 毛利率66%, 營收獲利皆優於預期與歷史新高, 毛利率符合
前次財測。展望第四季, 公司預估Q4營收523-543億美元, 營收中位數季增14%, 預估Q4毛利率65-67%, 持平並符
合預期, 但上調預估營利率至56.5-58.5%。此外, 台積電也將全年美元營收預估年增率從約40%, 升至44%。
• 2026全年預估資本支出維持不變, 保持600-640億美元。台積電未透露明年資本支出或擴廠具體計畫, 表示正在討

論德州廠規劃中。台積電重申, 2026-2028年的資本支出, 將比過去三年「更加顯著提高」。
• 預估台積電Q4 EPS可達31元以上, 全年EPS挑戰110元以上, 年增69%。

航運運費 油價走勢
原油｜地緣紛擾消息不斷, 油價高位震盪
• 面對11月即將到來的期中選舉壓力, 美國總統川普在燃料價格飆漲的逼迫下, 打出「聯俄壓油」牌, 不僅放寬制裁
向俄羅斯採購柴油, 更單方面高調宣布俄烏「能源停火」。然而, 克里姆林宮隨即轟炸烏克蘭能源設施, 戳破政治
秀的期待, 解決不了能源供應的近火。此外, 沙烏地基礎設施傳遭胡塞武裝組織轟炸, 更令市場擔憂。
• 雖然在期中選舉前, 川普雖可能持續透過政治干預、大釋戰備儲油來壓制油價, 但目前市場對這些政治動作似乎逐
漸鈍化, 加上航運運費持續攀高, 更不利能源價格放緩。
• 後市, 即便沙國以船對船等方式轉圜, 但地緣熱度難降、運費高踞, 期中選舉前政治壓力與原油供應的風險仍存, 油
價應仍維持高檔震盪格局。

營收獲利創新高, 毛利率走高 高毛利的服務營收大增為獲利動能關鍵
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2027Q1。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2027Q1。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。
• ASML第三季營收116.6億歐元, 年增55%, 高於財測高標, 淨利49.6億歐元, 年增70%, 雙雙創下歷史新高。值得注
意的是, 毛利率57.45%高於前季財測的56%, 主要是高利潤的基礎安裝管理(Installed Base Management)銷售優
於預期, 反映晶片與記憶體製造商對既有產線的擴產需求強勁, 明年毛利率有望透過漲價而提升。
• 公司管理層表示, 部分訂單能見度已達2029-2030年, 且原定的2028年擴產30%已無法滿足客戶需求, 將上調產能。
過去ASML因客戶集中度高, 缺少議價能力, 但隨先進製程與封裝晶片需求大增, 客戶數量與需求成長, 並且對既有

產線更新的需求提高, 將帶動ASML後續營收與獲利成長。

通膨壓力下, 家計支出呈現負成長 實質薪資與殖利率同步走高
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/30。
• 日本8月不含食物、能源的CPI年增1.9%, 但已公布的9月東京核心CPI(不含食物、能源)年增率由8月2%跳升至3%,
暗示日本9月CPI也呈上升趨勢, 這代表日本政府雖對油價進行大規模補貼 , 但民眾所承受的通膨壓力仍舊在上升 ,
家計支出也呈現連9個月負成長。
• 另一方面, 春鬥連三年漲薪5%以上, 實質薪資年增率終於在今年轉為正值, 民眾消費能力改善。隱含企業獲利好轉,
若搭配上日本10年期公債殖利率緩步走揚, 代表經濟呈現緩步復甦態勢。惟資金環境轉趨緊縮, 加上勞動力成本上
升, 除了AI相關企業獲利仍維持強勁成長動能外, 其餘行業將面臨消費承壓及融資成本上升的雙重挑戰。

半導體設備股7月以來表現分歧 設備股評價回落至3年平均水準
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
註: 取東京威力科創、愛德萬測試、迪思科平均。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 日本半導體設備廠受惠 AI需求爆發將享有長期成長題材 。兩大科技龍頭中, 東京威力科創主要是半導體前段製程
設備商, 產品涵蓋塗佈/顯影機、蝕刻機等; 愛德萬測試則在半導體後段, 特別是先進封測領域具關鍵地位。目前供
應鏈前段設備交期約在3~6個月, 先進封測則面臨嚴重供不應求、交期一再延後情形, 股價表現也出現顯著差異。
• 日本設備廠具全球領導地位, 享有較大盤更高的本益比。過去3年, 設備股平均PE33.8倍, 範圍介於15~45倍之間,
在半導體受惠AI需求的長期擴廠商機加持下, 獲利有望持續抬升, 若本益比低於平均水準, 則是逢低布局的機會。

三星電子2027-2028獲利仍有望持續成長 三大廠將持續提高HBM產能佔比
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2028Q4。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: J.P. Morgan預估, 截至2026/9/22。
• 三星電子第三季營業利潤率55%高於預期, 營業利潤107.4兆韓元則略低於預期, 主要受到韓元大幅升值影響 。此
外, 近期長鑫存儲發布首款4F²記憶體, 市場對於中國廠商擴產擔憂加劇, 使近期韓股表現弱勢。
• 然而, 產品發布到量產、良率合格仍需要很長的時間, 目前長鑫存儲整體技術仍落後三大廠2~3個世代。且高盛預
估中國記憶體需求至2028年將以50%的CAGR成長, 即便長鑫存儲積極擴產仍無法滿足中國自身需求, 市場對於中

國低價搶市佔的擔憂過大。此外, 美光先前財報會議時亦表示2027與2028的供需會較2026更加緊張, 三大廠持續
提高HBM產能佔比的趨勢不變, 記憶體廠未來兩年的獲利仍有持續成長的空間, 即便成長率放緩, 獲利維持時間會
遠較先前週期更長。

近期大部分市場參與者皆站在賣方 韓國個股槓桿ETF出現明顯資金流出
韓股｜回購買盤即將告終, 外資散戶同站賣方, 籌碼因素使韓股近期走弱
• 近期韓國股市走勢疲弱, 回檔幅度遠高於費半與台股, 除消息面的雜音外, 籌碼面也是原因之一。近期三星電子與
SK hynix的股票回購計畫即將額滿, 過去兩個月其它市場參與者皆站在賣方, 市場擔憂回購計畫結束後買盤將暫時
枯竭, 且韓國企業第三季的財報多數因匯率因素多數面臨下修, 在其他利空的干擾下加劇跌勢。
• 此外, 韓國個股槓桿ETF近期亦出現大量資金淨流出, 散戶在個股與槓桿ETF同步拋售, 顯示市場情緒偏向悲觀, 使
韓股評價已回落至歷史低值。然而, 即便未來兩年的獲利成長率將逐漸回落, 但在AI資本支出未歇的前提下, 韓國

企業的能夠維持在相對高位更久的時間(遠長於先前的循環), 現在進場買的是獲利的持久性而非成長率。但在股價
大幅修正後, 目前位階已具備較佳風險報酬比。

西班牙選舉民調 MSCI Spain vs Europe 權重拆解
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/5。 資料來源: MS, 截至2026/9/30。
歐洲｜西班牙選情利多金融股, 罷工風險成低接機會
• 民調顯示右派聯盟(PP、Vox)有望取得執政優勢(11/29大選), 市場預期新政府將取消銀行暴利稅、放寬金融監管並
重啟產業整併, 有利提升銀行獲利與資本運用效率。考量西班牙金融股占MSCI Spain權重高達45.5%, 遠高於歐洲
整體的26.9%, 在政策預期帶動下, 短期西班牙金融股乃至歐洲金融類股投資氣氛偏正向。
• 不過, 11月11日全國大罷工仍是重要觀察風險。近期民生壓力及勞資對立情緒升溫, 若抗議行動擴大, 可能影響之
後新政府改革推動速度。短期市場可望持續反映選舉紅利, 倘若罷工事件對金融股評價與歐股情緒的造成干擾, 加
上歐元區未來升息有利NIM, 反而是合適的切入點。

### Page 24

Cathay’s View
• 此前, 法國2027年財政預算案引發國會對峙, 法國CDS仍位於10月初以來的80點高位, 反映出市場對主權評級調降
與財政不確定性的政治風險溢價。然而, 這場債市風暴並非毫無防線。極右翼領袖馬琳·勒龐近期提出在2030年前
將赤字降至3%以下的影子財政計劃, 顯示即使政權可能輪替, 這為市場注入了關鍵的穩定劑 。預計信評機構下週
動作有限, 歐元短期應能止貶。
• 對歐元更核心的支撐 , 來自總體經濟的利差結構 。當前歐元區與美國的貨幣政策路徑並未出現實質性分歧 。隨著
兩大央行同步進入利率平台期或政策調整期 , 歐美升息與降息預期依然保持高度一致 。在雙方利差難以進一步擴
大的基本面大環境下, 歐元兌美元不具備單邊長期下跌的條件。
法國政治事件Timeline
資料來源: 媒體報導, 企業版ChatGPT、國泰世華整理, 截至2026/10/15 。
匯率｜法國預算展開討論, 歐元短期止穩

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 歐洲央行上周四(9/10)為年內第二次上調利率，以應對能源價格上漲帶來的通膨
壓力 ， 並警告物價壓力可能比先前預期更久 ， 推動市場加大對後續進一步收緊政
策的押注 。 歐洲央行總裁拉加德形容此次决定爲”無需多想的决定”， 並警告通膨
回落至 2%目標的時間點目前預計在 2027 年底。歐洲央行預期明年平均通膨爲
2.5%，高於 6 月的 2.3%；2027 年核心通膨預期爲 2.6%，高於此前的 2.5%。
⚫ 軍事消息人士稱，與伊朗結盟的胡塞武裝上周四(9/10)奪取葉門港口城市的控制
權 ， 並沿紅海海岸向南推進至戰略島嶼地區 。 美國總統川普表示 ， 他預計伊朗戰
爭將在美國中期選舉後結束。若胡塞武裝控制這條與 荷姆茲海峽同樣重要的水
道 ， 其支持者伊朗將在與美國的戰爭中獲得關鍵戰略優勢 ， 並進一步推高油價 。
⚫ 美國勞工部勞工統計局上週四(9/10)公布 8 月生産者物價指數 PPI 年增 5.4%，

高於市場預期的 5.3％。週五(9/11)公布 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 3.4%與前
月相同，核心 CPI 年增率 2.4%符合市場預期，月增率達 0.3%高於市場預期。
FED WATCH顯示數據公布後推升市場對於聯準會本周將升息的預期。
⚫ 日本央行數據顯示，8 月國內企業物價指數較去年同期上漲 7.6%。增幅高於市
場預估中值 7.4%。7 月漲幅經修正後爲 7.7%；8 月進口物價指數同比上漲 24.8%，
7 月修正後爲上漲 29.3%， 表明當月日圓貶值以及中東局勢影響推高進口成本和
整體通膨，同時鞏固市場對日本央行在 9 月會議進一步升息預期。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Sep/14/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.455，低點在 31.698，以 31.638 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 0.8 分。
上週台幣先升後貶，週初，受到市場對於日本央行 9 月升息的預期升溫，日圓
走升帶動美元指數跌破 99 整數價位低檔震盪 ， 加以台股高檔震盪 ， 外資延續匯入的
走勢，帶動台幣升破31.500 至 31.455 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月生產者物
價指數年增率由上個月的 4.8%上升至 5.4%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示聯準會
9 月升息機率攀升至 7 成，美元指數回到 99 上方，台股高點回落，外資偏向匯出，
台幣由升轉貶至 31.698 週內低點，終場在央行調節之下收在 31.638。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣區間震盪。國際市場方面，上週五公布美國 8 月核心消費者物價
指數月增率 0.3%高於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，提高本週聯準會升息的機率

至接近 9 成，此外中東局勢仍有升溫風險，油價持續高檔震盪，美元指數在 99 整數
關口盤整；就台灣市場來看，台灣 8 月出口衝上 824 億美元，創下歷年單月新高紀
錄，月增 9.4%，年增 41%，展現極為強勁的成長動能。顯示受惠於 AI 需求全面爆
發，帶動外銷出口暢旺，基本面強勁持續支撐台幣的表現；盤勢上，匯價逢低吸引
進口商及政府基金買盤進場，逢高則面臨出口商賣美金，且本週台美均有利率決策
會議，預估匯率波動將加劇。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間震盪。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.400-31.900

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/14/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週人民幣震盪走升。週初，雖然美伊戰火未平，帶動油價及美債利率高漲，
但市場對於日本央行 9 月升息預期升溫，日圓偏升帶動美元指數跌破 99 價位後低
檔震盪，人民幣延續偏升走勢，走升至6.7022 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月
生產者物價指數 5.4%優於市場預期 5.3%， 加以8 月核心消費者物價指數月增率 0.3%
優於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示本週聯準會 9 月升息預期大
幅攀升至近 9 成，美元指數回到99 上方，人民幣回吐部分週初以來的漲幅，終場境
外人民幣收在 6.7071，週線升值 8 點，境內人民幣則收在6.7079，週線升值 29 點。  
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間偏升。國際市場方面，美國通膨數據表現優於市場預期，
加以油價攀升至每桶 100 美元上方 ， 美債殖利率位居高檔 ， 美元指數也在99 上方盤
整，關注本週聯準會最新利率決議，預計在會議結果出爐以前，聯準會升息預期將
支撐美元指數，限制人民幣升值的空間。中國國內方面，人行中間價格緩步偏升，
上週升破 6.7800 價位，持續釋放人民幣偏強信號，支撐人民幣延續升值的方向。技
術面方面 ，USD/CNH 匯價居於各天期均線下方 ，且均線向下發散 ，加以匯價由低點
向上反彈測試 6.7150 關鍵價位後面臨壓力，顯示空方動能較盛，預計人民幣將延續
升值的態勢。綜合以上，預估本週人民幣走勢區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7300

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/14/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌 。週初 ，因市場預期日本央行將加快升息步伐及資金將
回流日本，使 USD/JPY 跌破至 155 整數關卡之下；週間，USD/JPY 延續跌勢最低
來到 152.87 後反彈，後續大致在 153 至 154 間波動；臨近週末，美國公布 8 月 PPI
及 CPI 數據顯示通膨仍處高位 ，USD/JPY 一度反彈至154.6 後回落 ， 終場收在153.54，
週線大跌 270 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週公布日本第二季 GDP
較上季增長 0.4%，季增年率也由 1.1%上升至 1.4%，主因為企業資本支出改善，而
7 月名目薪資年增 4.7%為 30 年來最大漲幅，而實質薪資年增 2.4%也為 5 年多來最
大漲幅，顯示日本經濟復甦力道較原先預期強勁，進一步強化市場對於日央升息的
預期，有利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月 CPI 及 PPI 數據顯示美國通膨仍處高位，強化
市場對聯準會升息預期，且中東局勢升溫，帶動油價突破 100 美元，預期將支撐美
元，限縮 USD/JPY 下行空間。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價多次向
下測試 153 整數關卡未能有效突破，顯示下方有買盤支撐，而 155 關卡附近則為前
波壓力位，因此短線先以區間看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的
可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：152.00-157.00

美元兌歐元匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 小幅下跌。 週初 ，EUR/USD 大致在 1.1600 至 1.1635 窄幅波動；
週間，市場等待歐洲央行利率決議及 美國通膨數據，EUR/USD 持續在 1.1615 至
1.1653 波動；週四晚間公布歐洲央行利率決議如預期升息 1 碼，而美國 8 月 PPI 高
於預期使市場對聯準會本月升息預期上升，EUR/USD 下行跌破 1.16 整數關口；臨
近週末，美國公布 8 月 CPI 顯示通膨仍具韌性，帶動 EUR/USD 走低來至 1.1568，
終場收在 1.1598，週線下跌 15 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週歐洲央行公布利率決
議如預期升息一碼，總裁拉加德表示即使面對能源價格衝擊，歐洲經濟第二季依然
展現韌性，預期第三季仍會延續，但未來通膨仍面臨上行風險，偏鷹派言論使市場
對於歐洲央行未來持續升息的預期上升，將帶給歐元支撐。二、美國 8 月 PPI 月增
0.4%符合市場預期 ， 年增5.4%高於預期 ，而 8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%年增 3.4%符合預期 ，
顯示通膨仍維持高位，使市場對聯準會升息預期增加，將支撐美元，不利歐元匯價
上行。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖一度突破 1.1630 年線但未
能站穩，顯示上方仍有賣壓，而下方1.1550 附近則有支撐，在有效突破前以區間震
盪看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1530-1.1680

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連日減發定存單 ， 使市場資金呈現平穩態勢 ， 且
資金需求不若先前，銀行間交投較為清淡，令短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.69%，利率較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週 5 年期指標券 A15108 成交在約 1.82%，10 年期指標券 A15106 成交在約
1.92%。 
美伊衝突升溫，帶動國際油價持續走揚，加劇市場對通膨之疑慮，加上美國8
月非農就業人數大增、8 月 PPI 年增率高於市場預期，市場預估聯準會下週升
息機率升至七成，帶動美債利率大幅反彈；台灣央行將於下週召開理監事會
議 ， 在台灣經濟成長與通膨維持高位下 ， 台灣央行面臨緊縮壓力 ， 預期台債殖
利率面臨走升壓力 ， 預估5 年券區間為 1.83%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.93%~2%。

Becky.HQ.Wang@yuanta.com

3Q26/4Q26 ABF、BT 稼動率持續向上，預期帶動營運成長 
南電 3Q26 主要成長動能來自於 ABF/BT，ABF 受惠 1.6T 高階交換器晶片及
AI ASIC 專案陸續放量，高階產品規格升級亦帶動平均銷售單價提升，其中
1.6T 交換器載板 ASP 相較前一代 800G 產品高出一倍以上。BT 載板則持續
受惠記憶體需求強勁。樹林廠及錦興廠的 ABF 與 BT 載板產能均已接近全面
滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率 60%–70%以上，進入 4Q26 有望趨於滿載。本中心
預估 3Q/4Q26 營收 163.8/188.5 億元，季增 20.6%/15.1% ，毛利率
30%/32.3%，稅後獲利 35/46.3 億元，EPS 5.43/7.16 元。 
玻纖布、CCL 持續漲價，預期 4Q26–2027 年載板價格上漲 
經本中心供應鏈訪查了解，高階 BT 材料已呈明顯供不應求，使部分 BT 料
件 Lead time 甚至拉長至 70 週以上；ABF 隨新世代 AI 載板朝 20 層以上
Build-up 結構發展，單顆 ABF 膜使用量同步提高，Ajinomoto 供應亦趨緊；
玻纖布方面， Nittobo 新增玻纖布供給已大致完成客戶分配，短期缺口難以
明顯緩解。隨單顆載板面積放大，每張 Panel 可排入的產品數量將快速下降，
持續消耗載板廠商有效產能。綜合來看，本輪載板漲價由規格升級、單顆材
料用量增加與高階有效產能不足共同驅動，預期 ABF/BT 載板於 4Q26 至
2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。 
2027 年營運動能明顯增強，給予買進評等，目標價 1,700 元 
南電於 8M26 董事會通過 468 億元資本支出計畫，主要鎖定大尺寸、高層數、
高精密度 IC 載板。在高階產品比重提升、稼動率維持高檔及報價上調帶動
下，預期 ABF/BT 毛利率可望持續改善，整體營運動能於 2027 年明顯增強。
本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1,095.4 億元，年增 49.3%/82.6%，
毛利率 26.9%/43.4%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。本中心給予南電買進評等，以
2027 年 EPS，30 倍本益比推得目標價 1,700 元。

元大觀點 
⧫  
台灣載板產能接近全面滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率提升至 60%–70%且
4Q26 有望滿載，預估 3Q/4Q26 營收季增 20.6%/15.1%。 
⧫  
規格升級與高階 產品及上游原料 產能不足共同驅動載板漲價，預期
ABF/BT 載板於 4Q26 至 2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。 
⧫  
高階產品比重提升、稼 動率維持高檔及報價上調，給予 南電 買進評
等，目標價 1,700 元，以 2027 年 EPS 56.78 元及 30 倍本益比推估。

營運分析 
3Q26 ABF、BT 稼動率持續向上，預期帶動營運成長 
南電 3Q26 營收 163.8 億元，季增 20.6%，年增 49.3%，主要成長動能仍來自於 ABF/BT，ABF 載
板方面，受惠 1.6T 高階交換器晶片及 AI ASIC 專案陸續放量，除出貨量持續增加外，高階產品規
格升級亦帶動平均銷售單價提升。其中，1.6T 交換器載板 ASP 相較前一代 800G 產品高出一倍以
上，隨高階產品占比提升，為營收與獲利成長的推力。BT 載板則持續受惠 DRAM 與 NAND Flash
等記憶體需求強勁，加上上游原材料成本上漲逐步反映於產品報價，使 BT 載板呈現量價齊揚。毛
利率預估為 30%，營業利益 43.7 億元，季增 50.6%，年增 604.1%，稅後獲利 35 億元，季增 55%，
年增 383.6%，EPS 5.43 元。

台灣廠全面滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率快速回升 
產能利用率方面，目前台灣主要載板廠區維持高檔運作，其中樹林廠及錦興廠的 ABF 與 BT 載板產
能均已接近全面滿載，顯示高階載板及記憶體相關需求仍相當強勁。同時，昆山 ABF 廠則為後續
營運改善的重要來源。隨美系網通大廠新專案自 7M26 正式進入量產，昆山 ABF 稼動率已由 2Q26
約 50%快速提升至 60%–70%，進入 4Q26 有望趨於滿載。伴隨稼動率提高及良率改善，昆山廠毛
利率亦顯著提升，預期後續貢獻幅度將提升。PCB 業務，稼動率正由 70%–75%向上，主因 AI 伺
服器板與記憶體模組板需求驅動。綜合上述，預期 4Q26 營收 188.5 億元，季增 15.1%，年增
68.8%，毛利率 32.3%，營業利益 55.1 億元，季增 26.1%，年增 472.1%，稅後獲利 46.3 億元，
季增 32%，EPS 7.16 元

玻纖布、 CCL 等價格仍持續漲價，預期 4Q26-2027 年載板價格上漲 
隨 AI GPU 、ASIC、高速交換器及 HBM 等應用快速成長，IC 載板規格持續朝大尺寸、高層數、細
線路、低翹曲及高耐熱發展，帶動材料由一般電子級產品轉向特種玻纖布、高階 BT 樹脂、ABF 增
層膜等高規材料。經本中心近期供應鏈訪查及日本材料展資訊整理，三菱瓦斯化學（ MGC）指出，
過去 BT 載板主要應用於 DRAM、NAND Flash 及消費性 IC，但隨 HBM、AI ASIC 、網通晶片與
CPO 導入，材料須進一步滿足低 CTE、高耐熱及高速電性需求，高階 BT 與傳統產品的規格差距
持續擴大，目前高階 BT 材料已呈明顯供不應求，部分料件 Lead time 甚至拉長至 70 週以上。玻纖
布方面，Nittobo 目前產能維持 100%滿載，雖規劃 2027–2028 年新增產能，但新增供給已大致完
成客戶分配，短期缺口難以明顯緩解。ABF 方面，隨新世代 AI 載板朝 20 層以上 Build-up 結構發
展，單顆 ABF 膜使用量同步提高，Ajinomoto 供應亦趨緊；同時 Resonac 相關載板材料報價已調
升約 20%–30%。綜合來看，本輪載板漲價並非單純景氣循環，而是規格升級、單顆材料用量增加
與高階有效產能不足共同驅動，預期 ABF／BT 載板於 4Q26 至 2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第4 頁，共 19頁  
圖 3：載板面積及層數增加亦在 Ajinomoto 未來載板發展藍圖

資料來源：Ajinomoto，元大投顧整理

南電此前已宣布 468 億元資本支出，為創立以來單筆最大投資 
南電於 8M26 董事會通過新一輪資本支出計畫，總投資金額達 468 億元，為公司成立以來最大單筆
投資案，規模亦超過 2018–2022 年上一輪擴產期間累計資本支出。其中約 110 億元投入廠房土建，
358 億元用於設備採購，新廠定位為具備 Class 100 無塵室等級的高階智慧工廠，主要鎖定大尺寸、
高層數、高精密度 IC 載板，以及 CoWoS、CoPoS 等先進封裝衍生需求。顯示此次投資並非既有
產線單純擴充，而是針對下一世代高階載板重新建置生產平台。整體而言 ，廠區調配方面，錦興廠
P2 升級已於 1H26 完成，主要提升自動化程度與無塵室規格，以因應大型、高階載板生產；樹林
廠則因應 2027 年美系客戶 Server CPU 需求成長，陸續將部分既有成熟產品轉往昆山生產，以釋
放台灣高階產能供新產品使用。

展望 2026-2027 年，ABF/BT 料源及載板的產能供應缺口將推升載板廠商營運 
AI 需求正在從「訓練」往「推論」延伸，這會讓晶片種類變多、數量變大，進而同時推升 IC 載板
的量與規格。此前從 Deep Learning 、Perceptual AI 一路到 Generative AI、Agentic AI，未來再往
Physical AI 發展。訓練（Learning）主要依賴大型模型運算，因此核心晶片以高效能 GPU 為主；
但到了推論（Inference）階段，更重視即時反應速度、功耗與成本，因此除了 GPU 之外，CPU 與
ASIC 的使用比例會明顯提高。展望 2026/2027 年，預計南電載板稼動率持續改善，其中 ABF 與
BT 均已進入高檔運作。ABF 方面，台灣樹林及錦興廠稼動率已接近滿載；昆山廠則為 2H26 稼動
率回升的主要來源，受惠美系網通客戶及中國客戶量產，ABF 稼動率由 2Q26 約 50%快速提升至
3Q26 的 60%–70%，預期 4Q26 在供給持續吃緊下，可進一步提升，2027 年初亦將因應美系客戶
Server CPU 需求快速成長。BT 載板供需則更加緊張，除現有 DRAM、SSD 相關需求持續強勁，
另一方面，部分同業逐步將 BT 產能轉向 ABF，韓系供應商亦優先支應集團內需求，市場有效供給
持續收縮，景碩亦表示稼動率也將逐步向上，預期 2027 年 BT 仍將維持高稼動率。 
綜合上述，本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1095.4 億元，年增 49.3%/82.6%，毛利率
26.9%/43.4%，營業利益 141.3/442.3 億元，年增 612.7%/213.1%，稅後獲利 117/366.9 億元，年
增 501.2%/213.5%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第5 頁，共 19頁  
產業概況  
AI 需求增溫造成 T-glass 玻纖布供應吃緊，助益近期 BT 載板報價調整 
低熱膨脹係數（Low CTE ）玻璃纖維布，簡稱 T-glass，為高階 AI ABF 載板及高階 BT 載板重要原
料，由 Nittobo(日東紡)獨家供應，並由日系廠商 Resonac(ABF 載板 CCL 主要供應商)、三菱瓦斯
(BT 載板 CCL 主要供應商)採用。由於高階 AI 晶片需求增溫帶動高階 AI ABF 載板出貨成長，造成
T-glass 供應吃緊並連帶使高階 BT 載板 CCL(終端用途包含記憶體控制器、手機 Ap、Sip 等)交貨
期拉長，T-glass 玻纖布供應正常時高階 BT 載板 CCL 交貨期約 8-10 週，後因供應吃緊造成交貨期
拉長至 16-20 週，目前因玻纖布供應更為緊張已無告知交貨期。 
經供應鏈調查，台系載板廠已於 2025 年 7 月開始針對部分採用 T-glass 之 BT 載板產品調漲價格，
此外 BT 載板亦有載板廠進行價格調漲，以反應高階玻纖布供應日漸緊缺，以及年初以來銅價、金
價等原物料成本上漲壓力。由於 BT 載板產業長期以來供過於求，且當前高階 AI 晶片普遍採用
ABF 載板，多數載板廠此前 BT 業務毛利率較為偏低，淡季時季毛利率多有承壓。本中心認為漲價
趨勢可延續，將有助載板廠獲利改善。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第6 頁，共 19頁

圖 6：金價近一年走勢

資料來源：Cmoney、元大投顧整理

中長期高階 ABF 載板價格或能進一步調升 
受惠 AI 發展浪潮帶動 AI GPU 、AI ASIC 等 AI 晶片需求成長，AI ABF 載板需求正向。本中心推測
短期高階 AI ABF 載板因獲利水準足夠暫無調漲壓力，但中長期隨需求持續上升、載板面積增大、
T-glass 供應持續吃緊，AI ABF 載板產品台系廠商後續仍有進一步議價空間。目前欣興、南電、景
碩皆有耕耘高階 ABF 載板，例如欣興在美系 GPU Blackwell 市佔率預估為 30-40%，且為全球主要
AI ASIC 載板供應商；南電今年 800G 載板比重預期約 7%，包含美系、非美系等終端需求；景碩
則有望成為美系 AI GPU 載板第三供。此外部分載板廠因原物料上漲等成本壓力在 2Q25、3Q25 小
幅調整中低階 ABF 載板報價，本中心判斷若 T-glass 供應持續吃緊並排擠 E-glass 產能，則後續中
低階 ABF 載板價格有望進一步提升。

圖 7：高階 ABF 載板供應鏈示意圖

資料來源：元大投顧整理

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第7 頁，共 19頁  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 91%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic) 、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國四大 CSP 資本支出將年增 91%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA GB300 的天下，AWS、Google、
Meta ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將
採購 TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，本中心預估 2026 年通用型伺服器出貨量將年增
23%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP 等業
者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中將消
耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 8

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第8 頁，共 19頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，NVIDIA
加 AMD GPU 預估年增 17%及 34%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用
GPU 算力需求仍維持成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者， 2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下， CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本， ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

圖 9：GPU/ASIC 出貨量預估

資料來源：元大投顧

AI 晶片面積持續擴大，將使 ABF 載板用量大增 
隨著大型語言模型與生成式 AI 的算力需求快速攀升，AI 晶片的設計持續朝向更高運算密度與更大
面積發展。無論是 NVIDIA、AMD 或各家大廠的新世代加速器，都呈現晶粒尺寸擴大、功耗上升、
I/O 腳位數倍增的趨勢。在此背景下，ABF 載板的需求不僅以「量」擴張，更以「規格」升級為主
軸。為支撐大型 GPU/AI ASIC 的高頻寬連接與多晶片架構，ABF 層數、線寬線距、孔徑控制及翹
曲管理均需同步提升，帶動高端載板技術門檻不斷拉高。由於先進 AI 晶片的封裝面積更大、電源
與訊號整合更複雜，單顆晶片所需 ABF 載板面積與材料用量明顯增加。整體而言，AI 晶片規模擴
大正驅動 ABF 產業進入新一輪升級周期，高階需求將成為未來成長的主要動能。

### Page 10

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第10 頁，共 19頁  
獲利調整與股票評價  
給予南電買進評等，目標價 1700 元 
本中心維持 2026–2027 年載板廠商將持續受惠於高稼動率與漲價趨勢的看法不變。主因新世代高
階載板規格持續大型化，傳統 PC／NB 載板尺寸多約 27×27mm，而 Server CPU 、AI ASIC 及高
速交 換器 載板 已逐 步 放大 至 70–80mm 、100mm 以 上， 部分 大型 產品 甚 至 朝 140×140mm 、
150×150mm 發展，未來更大型先進封裝架構更可能逼近 200mm 等級。由於載板生產係以固定尺
寸 Panel 進行製造，隨單顆載板面積放大，每張 Panel 可排入的產品數量將快速下降，同時邊角無
法有效利用所造成的 Area Loss 亦明顯增加，此將持續消耗載板廠商有效產能，有助產業維持高稼
動率，並支撐 2026–2027 年載板報價向上。本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1095.4 億元，
年增 49.3%/82.6%，毛利率 26.9%/43.4%，營業利益 141.3/442.3 億元，年增 612.7%/213.1%，
稅後獲利 117/366.9 億元，年增 501.2%/213.5%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。 
綜合上述，南電高階產品比重提升、稼動率維持高檔及報價上調帶動下，預期 ABF／BT 毛利率可
望持續改善，整體營運動能於 2027 年明顯增強。評價方面，南電歷史本益比交易區間約介於 6–36
倍；考量本輪 ABF／BT 供需結構改善、高階產品組合升級，以及稼動率快速提升所帶來的毛利率
擴張，本中心給予 2027 年 30 倍本益比評價，以 2027 年預估 EPS 推算目標價 1,700 元，並給予
買進投資建議。

公司簡介  
南亞電路板成立於 1997 年，前身為南亞塑膠自 1985 年開始營運的電路板事業部，1998 年正式獨立營運，並於 2006 年在
台灣證券交易所掛牌上市，為台塑集團旗下主要 PCB 與 IC 載板廠商，目前南亞塑膠持股約 67%。南亞電路板     年報
_8046 南電_2025 年報(1) 公司產品涵蓋傳統多層印刷電路板、HDI、軟硬複合板（Rigid-Flex），以及 ABF 與 PP／BT IC
載板，生產據點主要位於台灣錦興、樹林及中國昆山。南亞電路板 其中 IC 載板為公司主要高階產品布局，應用涵蓋伺服器
CPU、AI ASIC、高效能運算（HPC）、800G／1.6T 高速交換器、記憶體、SSD 控制晶片、SiP、行動裝置及車用電子等
領域；一般 PCB 則持續往 AI 伺服器、GPU 加速卡、伺服器與高階網通等高附加價值產品升級。    年報_8046 南電_2025
年報(1) 公司近年營運重心進一步向 AI 與 HPC 相關高階載板移動，受惠 AI 晶片朝大尺寸、高層數及高 I/O 規格發展，以及
CSP 持續擴大自研 ASIC 布局，南電持續強化高階 ABF 載板、1.6T 交換器及 AI 伺服器相關產品布局，並規劃新增高階載
板產能，以支應下一階段 AI 基礎建設需求。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第16 頁，共 19頁

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 南電整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低等風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於略為領先
的位置，在電子零組件行業的公司中排名略微領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 南電的整體曝險屬於低等水準，略為領先電子零組件行業的平均水準。  
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：南電在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。公司訊息揭露能力非常優良，
提供投資人及市場充足的資訊。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

Document 154: AI泡沫隐忧暂缓全球债券抛售潮，美债迎来今年以来最佳周表现

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI泡沫隐忧暂缓全球债券抛售潮，美债迎来今年以来最佳周表现.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

美债借助科技股暴跌带来的避险需求强势反弹， 10 年期收益率本周下⾏ 14 个基点⾄
5.11% ，创今年 2 ⽉以来最佳单周表现。 AI 投资可持续性疑虑引发资⾦转向政府债券，推动
全球债市逆转。尽管美国 9 ⽉通胀升⾄ 3.7% ，美联储表态缓和市场担忧，⾼收益率也持续
吸引⻓线资⾦，但 AI 相关借款⽅融资压⼒仍值得警惕。
摘要：
美国国债本周强势反弹，借助科技股抛售潮带来的避险需求，终结了持续数⽉的全球债券熊
市。
10 年期美债收益率本周下⾏ 14 个基点⾄ 5.11% ，创下今年 2 ⽉以来最佳单周表现。此前，受伊朗
地区冲突及美国经济韧性驱动，该收益率⼀度突破 5.3% ，触及 2002 年以来最⾼⽔平。本周转折
的核⼼催化剂，是市场对 AI 繁荣可持续性的担忧引发全球芯⽚股⼤跌，投资者随之涌⼊政府债券
避险。
此轮反弹同步缓解了欧洲主要经济体的融资压⼒。英国 10 年期国债收益率从本⽉早些时候逾
5.5% 的⾼位回落⾄ 5.39% ，意⼤利 10 年期国债收益率亦从近 4.75% 降⾄ 4.53% 。

避险情绪主导，科技股重挫触发债市逆转
本周债市的反转，直接源于科技板块的⼤幅调整。以科技股为主的纳斯达克 100 指数本周累计下
跌约 3% ，创 7 ⽉以来最差单周表现。数据中⼼相关债券价格下滑，令市场对 AI 投资热潮能否持续
产⽣疑虑，部分投资者将 AI 相关借款⽅所⾯临的处境称为 " 达尔⽂时刻 " 。
Aviva Investors 全球股票主管 Richard Saldanha 表⽰，科技股的上涨 " 在⼀段时间内确实有悖于
重⼒法则 " ，但更⾼借资成本的压⼒终究开始显现。
Jefferies 分析师 Mohit Kumar 指出， " 此轮⾏情由投资者对 AI 周期的担忧以及空头头⼨平仓共同驱
动。 " 这⼀机制意味着，此前押注债券继续下跌的投资者被迫回补，进⼀步放⼤了债市的反弹幅
度。
通胀数据冲击有限，美联储表态稳定预期
本周债市的回暖并⾮⼀帆⻛顺。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

美国 9 ⽉ CPI 数据显⽰通胀率升⾄ 3.7% ，中东冲突推⾼燃油价格是主要推⼿。数据公布后，交易
员⼀度⼤幅上调加息预期，周三市场⼀度定价未来⼀年将有四次 25 个基点的加息。
然⽽，随着抛售情绪从债市蔓延⾄股市，避险买盘迅速逆转了债券的跌势。美联储主席 Kevin
Warsh 周五在曼⾕国际货币基⾦组织峰会上表⽰，⾼于预期的通胀数据并未改变其对美国经济的
判断，进⼀步稳定了市场情绪。期货市场⽬前显⽰，未来 12 个⽉加息预期已回落⾄三次。
Royal London Asset Management 多资产投资主管 Trevor Greetham 表⽰， " 较⾼的收益率吸引
了政府债券买家，叠加科技股的避险情绪，以及市场认为美联储可能在 10 ⽉按兵不动的预期。 "

⾼收益率吸引⻓线资⾦，⼤型机构持续布局
此轮债市反弹，也折射出⾼收益率对⻓线资⾦的持续吸引⼒。近⼏个⽉来，美债已吸引⼤量买
盘涌⼊。资产管理巨头 Pimco 等头部机构明确表⽰，相较于⾼估值股票和收窄的信⽤利差，当前
债券收益率 " 极具吸引⼒ " 。
不过，借贷成本的整体上⾏仍对全球借款⼈构成持续压⼒，尤其是评级较低的企业。 AI 相关借款
⽅所⾯临的融资环境收紧，正成为市场密切关注的新⻛险点。本周数据中⼼债券的下跌，已向
市场发出了明确的预警信号。

Document 174: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-18).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

Hungary, Turkey and Kazakhstan will announce interest rate

Adam Antoniak, Peter Virovacz, Muhammet Mercan and Dmitry

Falling inﬂation has markets increasingly betting on a Turkish rate cut
Poland: Industrial output and labour market
data in focus
September industrial output and labour market data will be
released on Tuesday. Although the European and German
economies show signs of recovery, Polish manufacturing remains
fragile and uneven. Some sectors, including electrical equipment,
machinery and equipment, and other transport equipment, are
performing relatively well. Others, such as textiles, furniture and the
automotive industry, continue to struggle. With the calendar eﬀect
neutral (the number of working days is the same as a year earlier)
and a high comparison base, we forecast low single-digit growth in
industrial production in September. At the same time, we expect PPI

Poland’s labour market remains stable. It is no longer exceptionally
tight, nor showing signs of weakening. Wage growth in the
enterprise sector has moderated and is stabilising at around 6.0%,
no longer exerting signiﬁcant pressure on price growth.
Employment continues to decline amid an ageing population and
an insuﬃcient inﬂow of younger workers to replace those retiring,

particularly in manufacturing. Enterprise-sector data do not
capture the employment of immigrant workers.
Thursday brings the release of September retail sales data. Despite
some moderation, private consumption appears relatively resilient
to the current energy shock and high retail fuel prices. To some
extent, the impact of rising fuel prices on household ﬁnances is
being cushioned by government measures temporarily reducing
excise duty and VAT on fuels. Demand for durable goods remains

solid, while some restraint is visible in day-to-day spending on
necessities. Given the substantial savings accumulated in previous
quarters, households should be able to smooth their consumption
over time. Nevertheless, household consumption growth this year
is projected to be slower than in 2025.
Hungary: NBH expected to keep rates
unchanged
The National Bank of Hungary’s watchlist is fairly limited to ﬁscal
policy and risk sentiment. In practice, however, it is diﬃcult to

assess these factors in the run-up to the October rate-setting
meeting on Tuesday. Although inﬂation ﬁgures were dovish,
market volatility in relation to geopolitical developments remains
high. With no clear de-escalation in sight, risk sentiment won't
allow the NBH to cut rates now.
Regarding ﬁscal policy, although the central bank is aware of the
2027 budget, as the NBH governor sits on the Fiscal Council that
reviews and approves it, no public information will be available on

this. Therefore, we do not believe this will provide an opportunity for
immediate monetary policy easing. Given the timing, we anticipate

Turkey: Central bank expected to cut rates
by 100bp
Thursday brings Turkey's October interest rate decision. Recent
inﬂation data has boosted the likelihood of a rate cut.
In addition, investment fund volatility is likely to increase downside

risks to activity in the last quarter, while we expect the
macroeconomic impact to be manageable.
Given the September CPI reading and domestic demand weakness,
we expect the central bank to cut the policy rate by 100bp in the
October MPC meeting to 36%.
CCA: Kazakhstan expected to deliver
another rate cut
For the 23 October decision in Kazakhstan, a 25bp cut to 16.00% is
now our base case, as it meets the criteria we indicated after the
September rate decision: headline inﬂation slowed by 0.5pp to

9.3% YoY, households’ price expectations improved, and the tenge’s
strength continued to exceed expectations in September.
At the same time, a hold remains plausible, as the recent slowdown
in CPI was overwhelmingly food-driven, while a renewed
acceleration in overall inﬂation, taking it further away from the 5%
long-term target, remains a risk. Importantly, the global backdrop
of rising core rates also argues for keeping real rates high.
Regardless of the decision, we believe the scope for policy easing at

this point is limited.

Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 19 - 23
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation reports came in hot. September’s CPI rose 0.6%
m/m, lifting headline inflation from 3.4% y/y in August to 3.7%. Core CPI
rose 0.3% m/m, taking its y/y rate from 2.4% to 2.6%. Both topped
expectations. PPI final demand jumped 1.3% m/m. That pushed its y/y
rate to 6.3%, the highest since 2022. Retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, though
just 0.2% excluding gas stations.
Odds of a 25bps hike at the October 27-28 FOMC meeting fell from 22%

before the CPI report to 17%, despite the hot inflation data. Fed Chair
Kevin Warsh offered no signal on October's meeting at the IMF and World
Bank annual meetings in Bangkok on Friday. “I don’t prejudge meetings,”
he said. Odds of a December hike rose. We still expect the Fed to reverse
the remaining two of last year’s three 25bps “insurance” cuts. Fed officials
are now in their pre-meeting blackout period.
This week’s data calendar is light, with earnings season taking center

stage. Friday’s flash PMIs are the main US releases. The Fed’s Financial
Stability Report and Canada’s federal budget are tentatively due Tuesday.
Here’s more:
(1) ADP & Unemployment Claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a rise in the four-week average to 24,750 for the week ended
September 26, the highest since early June and up from a late-July
bottom of 8,250 (chart).
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 199,000, with the four-week

average at 198,500 (chart). Continuing claims fell to 1,710,000 for the

### Page 4

week ended October 2, matching their four-week average. The labor
market continues to improve.
(2) Flash PMIs. S&P Global’s flash M-PMI and NM-PMI for October (Fri)
follow September readings of 55.9 and 58.8 (chart). The services prices
component is worth watching after ISM’s NM-PMI prices-paid index
jumped to 70.7 in September, the highest since July 2022.
Flash PMIs for Australia and Japan (Thu) and for the Eurozone, Germany,
France, and the UK (Fri) also land this week. September’s M-PMIs were

52.9 for the Eurozone and 52.1 for the UK.
(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season picks up this week. Netflix,
GE Aerospace, and Northrop Grumman report on Tuesday. Tesla and
Meta, the first of the Magnificent-7 to report, follow on Wednesday along

### Page 5

with Lam Research, IBM, and Texas Instruments. Spotify, Bloom Energy,
Western Digital, Lockheed Martin, and Blackstone report on Thursday.
ASML and TSMC posted record Q3 results last week. ASML’s net sales
rose to €11.7 billion from €9.3 billion in Q2, with the gross margin up to
57.5% from 54.0%. TSMC’s revenue jumped 50.9% y/y. Analysts’
consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for S&P 500
companies of 24.0% y/y (chart).
(4) Global. China’s Q3 GDP (Mon) follows Q2’s 4.3% y/y growth (chart).

We expect a moderate pickup in activity. China’s unemployment rate for
September is also due, following August’s 5.2% rate.
UK CPI (Wed) for September follows August’s 3.1% y/y headline and
2.6% core (chart). Rising energy and input costs point to a further pickup,

### Page 6

with the input PPI up 8.2% y/y in Q2. Bank of England Governor Andrew
Bailey speaks later that day. UK labor market data (Tue) and PPI (Wed)
are also due.
Canada’s CPI (Mon) for September follows August’s 3.0% y/y headline
and 2.3% core (chart).

Document 238: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

### Page 2

11
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.

Document 240: GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Downgrading 'Go Global' To
Market Weight
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Robert Nyman / Unsplash
We are downgrading the “Go Global” portfolio strategy from overweight to
market weight and raising “Stay Home” (in the US) from underweight to
market weight.
Overseas earnings remain impressive, but the rallies in emerging market
economies’ stock markets have been led by just a handful of AI-related
stocks. Last week's rout in South Korea showed how vulnerable such
concentration leaves these stock markets. Additionally, European

economies look set to struggle as France's fiscal problems loom, and
China's economy depends almost entirely on exports. Plus, the Middle
East war could flare up again before or after the US midterm elections on
November 3, keeping oil prices, inflation, and interest rates elevated.
Here's more:
I. Global Bonds
The global bond rout took a breather last week. Yields fell in all eight
major bond markets we track, led by the US (chart). The US 10-year

Treasury yield fell 16bps to 5.08%, even as the CPI rose 3.7% y/y and the
PPI jumped 6.3% in September. That suggests bond investors see these
rates as attractive relative to their long-term inflation expectations.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields has stabilized at
around 137bps, near its recent high (chart). Spreads for Italy, Greece,

### Page 3

Spain, and Portugal have eased slightly from their late-September peaks.
Contagion from France has eased for now. But France's own fiscal risk
premium hasn't. The National Assembly began debating France's 2027
budget on October 13. Meanwhile, Paris is burning!
II. Global Stocks
Brazil leads the country ETF rankings mtd, up 12.3% in dollars, ahead of
next Sunday's presidential runoff (chart). South Korea is at the bottom,
down 17.7%. The US is up 0.1%, ahead of the All Country World ex-US

ETF, down 1.9%.

### Page 4

Europe's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has fallen to
13.0%, from as high as 30.8% in 2007 (chart). It looks set to keep losing
ground for a while. The US MSCI’s share has firmed to 64.8%, up from
62.4% in March. The Emerging Markets MSCI’s share has held at 12.1%
despite the Korean selloff.
The ratio of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI has turned
up in recent weeks (chart). It remains below its early-2025 high.

### Page 5

III. Earnings & Valuation
The All Country World ex US MSCI's forward earnings is up 41.4% y/y,
while forward revenues is up 11.0% (chart). The global economy is doing
well.
Overseas, earnings have far outpaced stock prices this year. The All
Country World ex-US MSCI is up 10.5% ytd, while its forward earnings is
up 35.9% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped 18.9% ytd.

### Page 6

The US MSCI's forward P/E is 19.1 versus 12.2 for the All Country World
ex-US, a 57% premium (chart). US profit margins justify about half of it.
The US forward profit margin is 16.8%, about a third higher than the All
Country World ex-US's 12.7%.
IV. China Focus
China remains a source of deflation for the global economy through its
massive trade surplus. Exports jumped 36.3% y/y in September to a
record high (chart). The trade surplus was $133.5 billion. Semiconductor

exports more than doubled during the first nine months of the year.
Booming exports are the only reason China's economy isn't imploding.

### Page 7

The domestic economy remains weak, weighed down by a rapidly aging
population and a property debacle. The Shenzhen Real Estate Stock
Price Index is testing a floor that has held since 2012 (chart). We would
worry if it broke below that level.
The stock market hasn't benefited from the export boom either, so Beijing
appears to be propping it up again. Regulators met on Thursday to
discuss stabilization measures, according to Bloomberg. They reportedly

urged companies and state funds to buy shares and asked some
investors to curb selling. Mainland A-shares, where Beijing's state-backed
funds do most of their buying, held up into mid-2026 before turning down
(chart). Offshore Chinese stocks have been falling since late 2025.

### Page 8

clause.

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

People Bank of China (October 20)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged next week . Industrial 
production is expected to edge up from 
5.2% yoy in August to 5.3% in September, 
supported by firm external demand. 
Exports stayed strong, rising 36.3% yoy 
in September, driven by the regional AI -
electronics upcycle.

Domestic momentum, however, remains 
uneven. Retail sales growth is expected 
to moderate to 0.3% yoy  in September. 
Household sentiment is weak amid 
uncertain job prospects, slower income 
growth and elevated precautionary 
savings, while subdued property prices 
continue to weigh on household wealth 
and consumption. Investment also stays 
weak, with fixed as set investment 
expected to contract further by 7.5% yoy 
ytd in September. Corporate investment 
is cautious amid the anti -involution 
campaign and geopolitical tensions, 
while the property sector continues to be 
a major drag.

Policymakers are likely to maintain a 
measured policy stance, relying on 
targeted fiscal support to sustain growth 
rather than further rate cuts.

Bank Indonesia and Bangko Sentral ng 
Pilipinas (Oct 21 and 22).

Bank Indonesia is expected to extend its 
pause, as they lean on non-rate measures, 
including intervention and hedging 
facilities to help the currency.  On Friday, 
MSCI signaled a  potential shift in 
Indonesia’s status to a standalone 
status, rather than a reclassification to 
frontier market , after consultations are 
complete in mid -2027. Any ensuing risk -
off move in the currency , would likely 
prompt greater central bank vigilance 
and intervention.

Our base case for the benchmark rate to 
be held unchanged this quarter , backed 
by a lower conviction around back -to-
back US rate hikes in Oct and relative 
rupiah stability. Separately, the 
government announced additional 
support for low-income households in 4Q 
by way of in -kind assistance and wage 
subsidies. This year’s fiscal deficit i s 
likely to close in at -2.85% of GDP, close 
to our forecast at -2.9%, but few 
uncertainties facing the 2027 math (note).

We expect a 25bp hike from the BSP to 
5.25% in October , after three rate 
increases earlier in the year. Rising by the 
highest pace in Asia, Philippines inflation 
surged to 7.2% y/y in September from 6.1% 
in August, matching this year's peak and 
approaching a three -year high.  Inflation 
risks remain tilted to the upside, driven by 
further public transport fare adjustments, 
elevated oil prices, recent wage hikes 
and peso depreciation. Fiscal space is, 
meanwhile, limited to support 
households, via subsidies and/ instate 
price caps. Governor Remolona also 
signalled that the BSP remains ready to 
act on rates to contain inflation. With 
inflationary pressures likely to be 
persistent, the likelihood of further hikes 
in 4Q26 rises.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Bank of Korea (October 22)

The Bank of Korea is expected to hold its 
policy rate at 3.00%, following back -to-
back rate hikes in July and August. AI -
driven growth momentum remains strong, 
as reflected in September export growth 
of around 80% YoY. However, the 
urgency for further tightening has eased, 
with CPI inflation stabilising at 2.9% YoY 
in September. The recent KOSPI 
correction and continued KRW strength 
also reduce the need for another near -
term hike. We maintain our call for a final 
25bp rate hike in November, bringing the 
policy rate to 3.25% by year-end.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Japan

September trade and CPI data are due 
shortly. Export growth is expected to 
remain solid at 24% YoY, supported by 
continued strength in AI -related 
semiconductor exports. CPI inflation is 
projected to rise notably to 2.6% YoY, 
driven by the phase -out of publ ic utility 
subsidies, higher global energy prices, 
and persistent inflationary pressures 
from a weak yen. The BOJ is likely to 
increasingly acknowledge that its 2% 
inflation target is within reach. However, 
it is expected to keep rates unchanged 
at 1.25% at its October 29–30 meeting to 
assess the impact of the latest rate hike 
in September.

Taiwan

September export orders and industrial 
production data are due shortly. Export 
orders are expected to sustain 60% YoY 
growth, while industrial production is 
projected to expand by 20% YoY. The 
global AI boom faces growing headwinds 
from hyperscalers’ cash flow pressures, 
central bank rate hikes, and higher bond 
yields. However, our high -frequency 
indicators across AI demand (token 
expenditure), investment (hyperscaler  
capex and data centre construction 
spending), and supply (semiconductor 
sales and exports) show no clear signs of 
a significant slowdown so far.

Malaysia

Malaysia’s external demand likely 
remained strong in September 2026, 
while inflation edged up slightly. We 
expect export growth to sustain its 
robust expansion at 45% yoy , with 
momentum anchored by strong demand 
for electrical and electronics goods. Oil 
& gas shipments likely remained buoyant. 
However, high base effects prevented a 
faster pace of expansion.

We expect headline inflation to rise 
modestly to 2.1% yoy in September 2026. 
The renewed increase in global oil prices 
likely contributed to higher increases in 
domestic energy costs, although fuel 
subsidies continued to limit the cost 
pass-through and keep underlying 
inflationary pressures contained. Food 
inflation likely also firmed, with adverse 
weather conditions associated with El 
Nino posing upside risks.

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s core and 
headline inflation to edge up slightly to 
2.3% yoy and 2.4% yoy , respectively, in 
September 2026, following modest 
increases to 2.2% yoy and 2.3% yoy in 
August. Inflationary pressures likely 
reflected firm private transport price 
increases amid higher petrol prices 
despite slower car price growth , the 
ongoing pass -through of higher global 
energy prices to fuel -exposed services 
items, positive contribution from 
elevated electricity & gas prices, firmer 
food inflation, and a slight increase in 
still-muted accommodation inflation. 
Firm, albeit not acc elerating, inflation in 
September 2026, which may be close to 
its peak in the Monetary Authority of 
Singapore’s assessment, likely validated 
its decision to maintain SGD NEER policy 
settings at the October 2026 review, 
following two proactive tightening 
moves in April and July.

Hong Kong

CPI is projected to edge up from 1.7% yoy 
in August to 1.9% in September, 
supported by improving consumption 
sentiment and higher global energy 
prices. Mainland visitor arrivals 
increased by 29.0% yoy in September on 
a daily average basis, providing furthe r 
support to retail spending and 
consumer-related services. Meanwhile, 
renewed conflict in the Middle East 
during the month has pushed up global 
energy prices, adding to upward 
pressure on utility and transport costs.

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor  
Email: allison.nathan@gs.com     
Tel: 212-357-7504   
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC

Higher bond yields: how concerning?

### Page 4
